        "text": "     1\t---\n     2\tname: frontend-design\n     3\tdescription: Create distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces with high design quality. Use this skill when the user asks to build web components, pages, artifacts, posters, or applications (examples include websites, landing pages, dashboards, React components, HTML/CSS layouts, or when styling/beautifying any web UI). Generates creative, polished code and UI design that avoids generic AI aesthetics.\n     4\tlicense: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt\n     5\t---\n     6\t\n     7\tThis skill guides creation of distinctive, production-grade frontend interfaces that avoid generic \"AI slop\" aesthetics. Implement real working code with exceptional attention to aesthetic details and creative choices.\n     8\t\n     9\tThe user provides frontend requirements: a component, page, application, or interface to build. They may include context about the purpose, audience, or technical constraints.\n    10\t\n    11\t## Design Thinking\n    12\t\n    13\tBefore coding, understand the context and commit to a BOLD aesthetic direction:\n    14\t- **Purpose**: What problem does this interface solve? Who uses it?\n    15\t- **Tone**: Pick an extreme: brutally minimal, maximalist chaos, retro-futuristic, organic/natural, luxury/refined, playful/toy-like, editorial/magazine, brutalist/raw, art deco/geometric, soft/pastel, industrial/utilitarian, etc. There are so many flavors to choose from. Use these for inspiration but design one that is true to the aesthetic direction.\n    16\t- **Constraints**: Technical requirements (framework, performance, accessibility).\n    17\t- **Differentiation**: What makes this UNFORGETTABLE? What's the one thing someone will remember?\n    18\t\n    19\t**CRITICAL**: Choose a clear conceptual direction and execute it with precision. Bold maximalism and refined minimalism both work - the key is intentionality, not intensity.\n    20\t\n    21\tThen implement working code (HTML/CSS/JS, React, Vue, etc.) that is:\n    22\t- Production-grade and functional\n    23\t- Visually striking and memorable\n    24\t- Cohesive with a clear aesthetic point-of-view\n    25\t- Meticulously refined in every detail\n    26\t\n    27\t## Frontend Aesthetics Guidelines\n    28\t\n    29\tFocus on:\n    30\t- **Typography**: Choose fonts that are beautiful, unique, and interesting. Avoid generic fonts like Arial and Inter; opt instead for distinctive choices that elevate the frontend's aesthetics; unexpected, characterful font choices. Pair a distinctive display font with a refined body font.\n    31\t- **Color & Theme**: Commit to a cohesive aesthetic. Use CSS variables for consistency. Dominant colors with sharp accents outperform timid, evenly-distributed palettes.\n    32\t- **Motion**: Use animations for effects and micro-interactions. Prioritize CSS-only solutions for HTML. Use Motion library for React when available. Focus on high-impact moments: one well-orchestrated page load with staggered reveals (animation-delay) creates more delight than scattered micro-interactions. Use scroll-triggering and hover states that surprise.\n    33\t- **Spatial Composition**: Unexpected layouts. Asymmetry. Overlap. Diagonal flow. Grid-breaking elements. Generous negative space OR controlled density.\n    34\t- **Backgrounds & Visual Details**: Create atmosphere and depth rather than defaulting to solid colors. Add contextual effects and textures that match the overall aesthetic. Apply creative forms like gradient meshes, noise textures, geometric patterns, layered transparencies, dramatic shadows, decorative borders, custom cursors, and grain overlays.\n    35\t\n    36\tNEVER use generic AI-generated aesthetics like overused font families (Inter, Roboto, Arial, system fonts), cliched color schemes (particularly purple gradients on white backgrounds), predictable layouts and component patterns, and cookie-cutter design that lacks context-specific character.\n    37\t\n    38\tInterpret creatively and make unexpected choices that feel genuinely designed for the context. No design should be the same. Vary between light and dark themes, different fonts, different aesthetics. NEVER converge on common choices (Space Grotesk, for example) across generations.\n    39\t\n    40\t**IMPORTANT**: Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision. Maximalist designs need elaborate code with extensive animations and effects. Minimalist or refined designs need restraint, precision, and careful attention to spacing, typography, and subtle details. Elegance comes from executing the vision well.\n    41\t\n    42\tRemember: Claude is capable of extraordinary creative work. Don't hold back, show what can truly be created when thinking outside the box and committing fully to a distinctive vision.\n    43\t",

Images of spaces and buildings that affect him are every bit as important as particular pieces of music or books that inspire him. \n\nFrom the composition and \u201cpresence\u201d of the materials to the handling of proportions and the effect of light, this poetics of architecture enables the reader to recapitulate what really matters in the process of house design. Materials react with one another and have their radiance, so that the material composition gives rise to something unique. Material is endless. If I can find a meaningful way of interlocking and superimposing these qualities, the object will assume a depth and richness. If I am to achieve this effect, the qualities I am giving the design must merge and blend with the constructional and formal structure of the finished building.\n\n\n\n\u5efa\u7b51\u7ed8\u56fe\u8bd5\u7740\u5c3d\u53ef\u80fd\u51c6\u786e\u5730\u8868\u8fbe\u5efa\u7b51\u7684\u6c1b\u56f4\uff0c\u5728\u5176\u9884\u8bbe\u4e4b\u5904\u3002\u4f46\u662f\uff0c\u7ed8\u56fe\u6210\u679c\u5e38\u5e38\u7528\u6765\u5f3a\u8c03\u90a3\u4e2a\u5b9e\u9645\u5bf9\u8c61\u7684\u4e0d\u5728\u573a\uff0c\u4e4b\u540e\u663e\u73b0\u7684\u662f\u5bf9\u4efb\u4f55\u4e00\u79cd\u7ed8\u56fe\u4e0d\u5145\u5206\u7684\u610f\u8bc6\u3001\u5bf9\u5b83\u6240\u627f\u8bfa\u7684\u4e8b\u5b9e\u7684\u597d\u5947\uff0c\u4e5f\u8bb8\u8fd8\u6709\u2014\u2014\u5982\u679c\u8fd9\u627f\u8bfa\u6709\u80fd\u6253\u52a8\u6211\u4eec\u7684\u529b\u91cf\u2014\u2014\u5bf9\u5b83\u5b58\u5728\u7684\u6e34\u671b\u3002 If the naturalism and graphic virtuosity of architectural portrayals are too great, if they lack \u201copen patches\u201d where our imagination and curiosity about the reality of the drawing can penetrate the image, the portrayal itself becomes the object of our desire, and our longing for its reality wanes because there is little or nothing in the representation that points to the intended reality beyond it.\n\nWhile I am designing I try to find out what these images mean so that l can learn how to create a wealth of visual forms and atmospheres. \n\n\u505a\u8bbe\u8ba1\u7684\u65f6\u5019\uff0c\u6211\u4f1a\u5141\u8bb8\u81ea\u5df1\u88ab\u8bb0\u5fc6\u4e2d\u7684\u56fe\u50cf\u548c\u60c5\u7eea\u5f15\u5bfc\uff0c\u5b83\u4eec\u80fd\u5173\u8054\u5230\u6211\u6240\u5bfb\u6c42\u7684\u5efa\u7b51\u3002\u6d6e\u73b0\u8111\u6d77\u7684\u5927\u591a\u6570\u56fe\u50cf\u6765\u81ea\u6211\u4e2a\u4eba\u7684\u4e3b\u89c2\u7ecf\u9a8c\uff0c\u800c\u6781\u5c11\u4f34\u6709\u5efa\u7b51\u8bf4\u660e\u3002\u8bbe\u8ba1\u65f6\uff0c\u6211\u8bd5\u56fe\u627e\u51fa",

        "text": "- Born: Juhani Uolevi Pallasmaa 14 September 1936 H\u00e4meenlinna, Finland\n- Occupation: Architect\n- Practice: Arkkitehtitoimisto Juhani Pallasmaa KY\n- Buildings: Art Museum, Rovaniemi Institut Finlandais, Paris Kamppi Centre, Helsinki Moduli 225\n- Projects: Tapiola development plan\n- Design: Helsinki Telephone Association, phone booth\n\nJuhani Uolevi Pallasmaa (born 14 September 1936 in\n[H\u00e4meenlinna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H%C3%A4meenlinna), Finland) is a [Finnish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finnish_people) architect and former professor of architecture and dean at the [Helsinki University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki_University_of_Technology).\nAmong the many academic and civic positions Pallasmaa has held are those of Director of the Museum of Finnish Architecture 1978\u20131983, and head of the Institute of Industrial Arts,\n[Helsinki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki). \n\n[[3]](./Juhani_Pallasmaa#cite_note-3) Pallasmaa has also lectured widely in Europe, North and South America, Africa and Asia.\nHis exhibitions of Finnish architecture, planning and visual arts have been displayed in more than thirty countries and he has written numerous articles on cultural philosophy,\n[environmental psychology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_psychology) and theories of architecture and the arts. Many of his articles are first featured in ARK (The Finnish Architectural Review).\nAmong Pallasmaa's many books on architectural theory is The Eyes of the Skin \u2013 Architecture and the Senses, a book that has become a classic of architectural theory and is required reading on courses in many schools of architecture around the world.\nA selection of essays written by Pallasmaa, from the early years to more recent ones, has been translated into English and collated together in the book Encounters \u2013 Architectural Essays (Helsinki, 2005), edited by Peter MacKeith. \n\n[Finnish Association of Architects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finnish_Assoc",

According to Article 9 of the \"What should be recorded and what should not be recorded in the formal contract for art and cultural performance tickets\" stipulated by the Ministry of Culture, the application and handling fees for refund and exchange of tickets, The execution unit follows \"Option 1: Tickets cannot be refunded within 10 days before the performance, and the refund fee is capped at 10% of the face price.\"  \n2. If you purchase tickets within 10 days before the event and apply for a credit card dispute refund, 10% of the ticket value will be refunded, excluding the ticket collection fee. The organizer follows the refund regulations set by the Ministry of Culture. The non-refundable 90% of the amount is as follows: your ticket location will be retained, and the administrative processing and event will be held as scheduled without any changes.  \n3. Please do not purchase tickets from unauthorized ticket sales channels, websites or places from unknown sources to avoid damaging your rights and interests.  \n4. Please keep event tickets properly. If they are damaged, lost, or difficult to identify clearly, they will not be reissued and admission will not be possible.  \n5. Tickets are required for admission to this event. One person has one vote. An activity bracelet will be provided after ticket verification.  \n6. If you want to change the date or ticket type after the order is confirmed, please upgrade your ticket on-site at the event.\n\n\n**\u270b Entry Rules**\n\n1. Wristband Required: You must wear your wristband at all times for identification. Anyone found without one will be charged the full on-site ticket price.  \n2. Two-Day Passes: Do not remove or damage your wristband after Day 1. It is required for Day 2 entry. Broken or tampered wristbands are invalid; a new ticket will be required.  \n3. Identity Verification: Facial recognition is used at the Two-Day Pass entrance. Tickets are non-transferable and for single-person use only.  \n4. No Re-entry: Leaving the venue voids your wristband (it will be removed). To re-enter, you must purchase a new ticket.\n\n\n**\ud83d\udeab Items prohibited within the venue**\n\n1. In accordance with the requirements of the Civil Aviation Authority, the following items are prohibited within the venue: \"fireworks, balloons, drones, laser pointers\". Offenders will be fined by the Civil Aviation Authority in accordance with the law.  \n2. Various types of umbrellas, flagpoles, telescopic selfie sticks,  benches, chairs, recliners, folding chairs, tents, knives, sticks, glass bottles, and any items with offensive characteristics.  \n3. Any illegal items, drugs, flammable materials, explosives, high-pressure spray cans, and items with unclear markings.  \n4. All cigarettes (opened/unopened), cigars, matches, lighters, electronic cigarettes, heated tobacco products, and tobacco oil (smoking areas and tobacco sales will be provided within the venue).  \n5. Any beverages or food items (including betel nuts, chewing gum, tea bags, candies, or chocolates).  \n6. Any containers (water bags, thermos bottles, food containers, ice buckets, etc.).  \n7. Various types of water guns (available for purchase within the event venue).  \n8. Large professional photographic equipment (except with prior approval from the organizer).  \n\u203b The organizer reserves the right of final interpretation. Discovery of the above prohibited items will be considered voluntary abandonment.\n\n\n**\u203c\ufe0f Notes**\n\n1. Participants must be at least 18 years old to attend this event. ID verification will be conducted upon entry. Please carry a government-issued identification document (national ID card, driver's license, passport), and only physical documents will be accepted for verification.  \n2. Please drink responsibly and adhere to the principle of \"Don't drink and drive, don't drive after drinking\" while at the event venue. If you consume alcohol, please use public transportation to leave the venue.  \n3. To ensure the safety of the event, there will be on-site police patrols. If you observe any suspicious, dangerous, or rule-violating behavior, or if you experience any discomfort, please immediately notify nearby staff or visit the medical station for assistance.  \n4. The organizer reserves the right to request the removal of individuals who violate rules or severely disturb others. Re-entry will be prohibited, and no refunds will be provided by the organizer.  \n5. To ensure smooth entry and exit traffic flow, it is recommended to use public transportation. Free shuttle services will be provided for the event.  \n6. For safety reasons, security and staff will inspect bags and conduct body searches upon entry.  \n7. If you carry medical drugs, please clearly label them and bring proof of medication. On-site medical personnel will assess whether they can be brought into the venue.  \n8. The event stage will feature multiple laser lights. For your safety, please be extra cautious and avoid direct eye contact with the lights.  \n9. Bags larger than A3 paper and any suitcases must be stored in the designated area outside the venue.  \n10. Pets (excluding service dogs) are not allowed at the event.  \n11. After the event, if any identification documents, health insurance cards, financial (credit) cards, or other items are found on-site, the organizer will photograph and post all lost items on the official social media.  \n12. In the event of natural disasters or force majeure, the organizer reserves the right to make temporary adjustments or cancel the event, and will handle related refunds in accordance with regulations.  \n13. In accordance with the standardized contract for cultural and arts performance tickets, if there are changes to the main performances or programs, consumers may request a full refund before the performance. Any changes to the program or performances will be promptly announced on the official website, along with reasons, and the refund process will be announced within seven days of the announcement (refunds will be completed within 90 days of refund application submission, with deferment to the next working day if it falls on a holiday).  \n14. Credit card purchases are accepted for this event. In case of event cancellation, postponement, or changes to major performances or artists, refunds can be requested from the original credit card issuer.  \n15. The organizer will record the event through photography and videography. Single-day GA companion tickets and love tickets can only be purchased on-site at the event. Each ticket isoffered at a half-price discount of on-site ticket price.  \n17. To purchase a love ticket, one must hold a valid \"Republic of China Disability Certificate\" that is still valid on the day of the event. The companion ticket can only be purchased if the disability certificate indicates the \"necessary companion discount measure.\" Each disability certificate holder can purchase a maximum of one love ticket and one companion ticket per event (provided the certificate includes the necessary annotation). The original disability certificate must be presented upon entry, and the companion must enter the venue with the disabled person. If the entry requirements are not met or if the individual does not hold a valid disability certificate, entry may be denied, and no ticket refunds or exchanges will be issued.  \n18. The refund policy for this event adopts the following explanation under scheme one: Refunds for this event will be handled by the executing unit. Refunds are not permitted within 10 days prior to the performance, and the maximum processing fee for refund or exchange is 10% of the ticket face value.  \n\nHowever, if consumers purchase tickets before the deadline but receive them after the deadline or have not received them by the start of the performance, they are still eligible for a refund. **02 \u30fb Be a host.**\n## **\u7576\u4e00\u500b\u4e3b\u4eba,\u4e0d\u662f\u54e1\u5de5\u3002**\n\n### \u610f\u601d\n\u4f60\u8d70\u9032 ESTIA         "text": "     1\t---\n     2\tname: docx\n     3\tdescription: \"Use this skill whenever the user wants to create, read, edit, or manipulate Word documents (.docx files) or Word templates (.dotx files). Triggers include: any mention of 'Word doc', 'word document', '.docx', '.dotx', or requests to produce professional documents with formatting like tables of contents, headings, page numbers, or letterheads. Also use when extracting or reorganizing content from .docx or .dotx files, inserting or replacing images in documents, performing find-and-replace in Word files, working with tracked changes or comments, or converting content into a polished Word document. If the user asks for a 'report', 'memo', 'letter', 'template', or similar deliverable as a Word or .docx file, use this skill. Do NOT use for PDFs, spreadsheets, Google Docs, or general coding tasks unrelated to document generation.\"\n     4\tlicense: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms\n     5\t---\n     6\t\n     7\t# DOCX creation, editing, and analysis\n     8\t\n     9\tA `.docx` is a ZIP archive of XML files. Choose your approach by task:\n    10\t\n    11\t| Task | Approach |\n    12\t|---|---|\n    13\t| **Create** a new document | Write a `docx` (npm) script \u2014 see gotchas below |\n    14\t| **Edit** an existing document | `unzip` \u2192 edit `word/document.xml` \u2192 `zip` (docx-js cannot open existing files) |\n    15\t| **Read** content | `pandoc -t markdown file.docx` |\n    16\t\n    17\t> Script paths below are relative to this skill's directory.\n    18\t\n    19\t## Creating with docx-js \u2014 gotchas\n    20\t\n    21\tInstall: `npm install docx`. The model knows the API; these are the footguns:\n    22\t\n    23\t- **Page size defaults to A4.** For US Letter set `page: { size: { width: 12240, height: 15840 } }` (DXA; 1440 = 1\u2033).\n    24\t- **Landscape:** pass portrait dimensions and `orientation: PageOrientation.LANDSCAPE` \u2014 docx-js swaps width/height internally.\n    25\t- **Tables need dual widths:** set `columnWidths` on the table AND `width` on every cell, both in `WidthType.DXA` (PERCENTAGE breaks in Google Docs). Column widths must sum to the table width.\n    26\t- **Table shading:** use `ShadingType.CLEAR`, never `SOLID` (renders black).\n    27\t- **Lists:** never insert `\u2022` literally; use a `numbering` config with `LevelFormat.BULLET`.\n    28\t- **`ImageRun` requires `type:`** (`\"png\"`, `\"jpg\"`, \u2026).\n    29\t- **`PageBreak` must be inside a `Paragraph`.**\n    30\t- **Never use `\\n`** \u2014 use separate `Paragraph` elements.\n    31\t- **TOC:** headings must use built-in `HeadingLevel.*`; custom heading styles need `outlineLevel` set or they won't appear.\n    32\t- **Don't use a table as a horizontal rule** \u2014 use a paragraph bottom border instead.\n    33\t- **Dot-leader / right-aligned-on-same-line:** use `PositionalTab` (`alignment: PositionalTabAlignment.RIGHT`, `leader: PositionalTabLeader.DOT`) inside a `TextRun`, not literal `.` or space padding.\n    34\t\n    35\t## Verify the output\n    36\t\n    37\tAfter writing a `.docx`, render it and look at it:\n    38\t\n    39\t```bash\n    40\tpython scripts/office/soffice.py --headless --convert-to pdf output.docx\n    41\tpdftoppm -jpeg -r 100 output.pdf page\n    42\tls page-*.jpg   # then Read the images\n    43\t```\n    44\t\n    45\t`pdftoppm` zero-pads page numbers to the width of the page count (`page-01.jpg`\u2026`page-12.jpg`).\n    46\t\n    47\t## Editing existing documents\n    48\t\n    49\tLegacy `.doc` files must be converted first: `python scripts/office/soffice.py --headless --convert-to docx file.doc`.\n    50\t\n    51\t```bash\n    52\tunzip -q doc.docx -d unpacked/\n    53\tfind unpacked -type l -delete   # strip symlink entries \u2014 docx from external parties is untrusted\n    54\t# edit unpacked/word/document.xml in place \u2014 do NOT reformat or pretty-print\n    55\t(cd unpacked && rm -f ../out.docx && zip -Xr ../out.docx .)\n    56\t```\n    57\t\n    58\t**Tracked changes:** wrap runs in `<w:ins>`/`<w:del>` with `w:id`, `w:author`, `w:date` attributes. Inside `<w:del>`, the text element is `<w:delText>`, not `<w:t>`.\n    59\t\n    60\tTo produce a clean copy with all tracked changes accepted: `python scripts/accept_changes.py in.docx out.docx`.\n    61\t\n    62\t## Comments\n    63\t\n    64\tComments require six cross-linked files. Use the helper \u2014 directory mode when you'll also be editing `document.xml` (saves an unzip/rezip cycle), `.docx`-direct mode otherwise:\n    65\t\n    66\t```bash\n    67\t# Against an already-unpacked directory (preferred when also placing markers)\n    68\tpython scripts/comment.py unpacked/ \"Fees & expenses cap is too low\"\n    69\tpython scripts/comment.py unpacked/ \"Agreed\" --parent 0\n    70\t\n    71\t# Against a .docx directly\n    72\tpython scripts/comment.py contract.docx \"This cap is too low\" -o annotated.docx\n    73\t```\n    74\t\n    75\tThe script writes `comments.xml`, `commentsExtended.xml`, `commentsIds.xml`, `commentsExtensible.xml`, the relationships, and the content-type overrides. Comment IDs are auto-assigned. It then prints the `<w:commentRangeStart>`/`<w:commentRangeEnd>`/`<w:commentReference>` snippet to add to `word/document.xml` so the comment anchors to specific text \u2014 until you place those markers, the comment exists but is not visible.\n    76\t\n    77\t## Dependencies\n    78\t\n    79\t`docx` (npm) \u00b7 `pandoc` \u00b7 LibreOffice (`soffice`) \u00b7 `pdftoppm` (Poppler)\n    80\t",

FONT_SANS,\n        size: 18,\n        color: COLOR_ACCENT,\n        characterSpacing: 100,\n        bold: true,\n      }),\n    ],\n  });\n}\n\nfunction chapterTitle(text) {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    alignment: AlignmentType.LEFT,\n    heading: HeadingLevel.HEADING_1,\n    spacing: { before: 100, after: 400 },\n    children: [\n      new TextRun({\n        text,\n        font: FONT_SERIF,\n        size: 48,\n        color: COLOR_INK,\n      }),\n    ],\n  });\n}\n\nfunction sectionTitle(text) {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    alignment: AlignmentType.LEFT,\n    heading: HeadingLevel.HEADING_2,\n    spacing: { before: 360, after: 180 },\n    children: [\n      new TextRun({\n        text,\n        font: FONT_SERIF,\n        size: 28,\n        color: COLOR_INK,\n        bold: true,\n      }),\n    ],\n  });\n}\n\nfunction label(text) {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    alignment: AlignmentType.LEFT,\n    spacing: { before: 240, after: 100 },\n    children: [\n      new TextRun({\n        text,\n        font: FONT_SANS,\n        size: 16,\n        color: COLOR_ACCENT,\n        characterSpacing: 120,\n        bold: true,\n      }),\n    ],\n  });\n}\n\nfunction body(text) {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    alignment: AlignmentType.LEFT,\n    spacing: { before: 120, after: 120, line: 340 },\n    children: [\n      new TextRun({\n        text,\n        font: FONT_SERIF,\n        size: 22,\n        color: COLOR_INK,\n      }),\n    ],\n  });\n}\n\nfunction quote(text) {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    alignment: AlignmentType.LEFT,\n    spacing: { before: 200, after: 200, line: 340 },\n    indent: { left: 500 },\n    children: [\n      new TextRun({\n        text,\n        font: FONT_SERIF,\n        italics: true,\n        size: 24,\n        color: COLOR_INK,\n      }),\n    ],\n  });\n}\n\nfunction bullet(text) {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    alignment: AlignmentType.LEFT,\n    spacing: { before: 80, after: 80, line: 320 },\n    indent: { left: 400, hanging: 200 },\n    children: [\n      new TextRun({\n        text: \"\u00b7 \",\n        font: FONT_SERIF,\n        size: 22,\n        color: COLOR_ACCENT,\n      }),\n      new TextRun({\n        text,\n        font: FONT_SERIF,\n        size: 22,\n        color: COLOR_INK,\n      }),\n    ],\n  });\n}\n\nfunction valueHeader(number, englishTitle, chineseTitle) {\n  return [\n    new Paragraph({\n      spacing: { before: 500, after: 60 },\n      children: [\n        new TextRun({\n          text: number,\n          font: FONT_SANS,\n          size: 20,\n          color: COLOR_ACCENT,\n          bold: true,\n          characterSpacing: 100,\n        }),\n      ],\n    }),\n    new Paragraph({\n      spacing: { before: 60, after: 40 },\n      children: [\n        new TextRun({\n          text: englishTitle,\n          font: FONT_SERIF,\n          size: 36,\n          color: COLOR_INK,\n          bold: true,\n        }),\n      ],\n    }),\n    new Paragraph({\n      spacing: { before: 40, after: 240 },\n      children: [\n        new TextRun({\n          text: chineseTitle,\n          font: FONT_SERIF,\n          italics: true,\n          size: 26,\n          color: COLOR_SOFT,\n        }),\n      ],\n    }),\n  ];\n}\n\nfunction divider() {\n  return new Paragraph({\n    alignment: AlignmentType.CENTER,\n    spacing: { before: 240, after: 240 },\n    children: [\n      new TextRun({\n        text: \"\u00b7 \u00b7 \u00b7\",\n        font: FONT_SERIF,\n        size: 24,\n        color: COLOR_ACCENT,\n      }),\n    ],\n  });\n}\n\nfunction pageBreak() {\n  return new Paragraph({ children: [new PageBreak()] });\n}\n\nfunction spacer(size = 400) {\n  return new Paragraph({ spacing: { before: size, after: size }, children: [] });\n}\n\n//  Document Content\n// A server ends when the shift ends; a host is who you are all the way home.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Read it before every message. Before every call. Before every guest arrival. It is the difference between us and everyone else.\"));\n\nchildren.push(sectionTitle(\"Brand Promise \u00b7 for the guest, external\"));\nchildren.push(spacer(200));\nchildren.push(quote(\"Every detail, a welcome.\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"\u6bcf\u4e00\u500b\u7d30\u7bc0,\u90fd\u662f\u4e00\u6b21\u6b61\u8fce\u3002\"));\nchildren.push(spacer(200));\nchildren.push(body(\"This is the sentence we say to the world. Only this: everything we do is a form of welcoming you.\"));\n\nchildren.push(sectionTitle(\"Signature Line \u00b7 the brand's autograph\"));\nchildren.push(spacer(200));\nchildren.push(quote(\"Warmth, made in Taiwan.\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"\u6eab\u5ea6,\u7522\u81ea\u53f0\u7063\u3002\"));\nchildren.push(spacer(200));\nchildren.push(body(\"This is our proudest sentence. It carries a double meaning \u2014 warmth of design, warmth of service. It is used in interviews, on the About page of the website, in investor conversations, and read aloud to every new team member on their first day.\"));\nchildren.push(spacer(200));\n\n// Origin story\nchildren.push(quote(\"When I was travelling in Greece, I realised something.\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"I have been to many countries. So why did Greece stay with me?\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"Of course, the white houses were beautiful. The marble, the sunlight, the sea \u2014 Greece has all of it.\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"But what makes Greece truly Greece \u2014 what lifts it to another level \u2014 is the hospitality of its people. The way they welcomed me, like family, with no agenda.\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"When I came home, I thought: this is what luxury really is. Not marble. Not designer furniture. It's a welcome that feels true.\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"So I named my brand after Estia \u2014 the Greek goddess of hearth and home \u2014 and set out to build something in Taiwan that carries that same warmth.\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"ESTIA is Greek in spirit. Taiwanese in hands.\"));\nchildren.push(quote(\"That's the whole idea.\"));\n\nchildren.push(pageBreak());\n\n// \u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\n// CHAPTER 7 \u2014 DAY 7: THE VALUES\n// \u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\u2550\n\nchildren.push(chapterNumber(\"DAY SEVEN \u00b7 THE VALUES\"));\nchildren.push(chapterTitle(\"Eight beliefs. Eight policies.\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"What follows is the constitution of ESTIA Residences. Six values for every team member. Two values for the founder to safeguard on behalf of the brand. Each is paired with the policies that make it real.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"These are not rules. Rules tell you what you must do. These tell you what you would want to do \u2014 if you understood who we are.\"));\n\nchildren.push(divider());\n\nchildren.push(body(\"The first six values are for every host. They shape how we think, decide, and speak \u2014 every day, in every interaction.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 01 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"01\", \"Intention over action.\", \"\u610f\u5716,\u5148\u65bc\u52d5\u4f5c\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"What guests feel is not what we do \u2014 it is why we do it. Before every call, every message, every arrival, pause for thirty seconds and ask yourself one question: am I doing this to help him, or to get it done? Those thirty seconds are the difference between ESTIA and everyone else.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"WHY THIS IS FIRST\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Every other value is an action \u2014 smile, remember, respond, notice. Actions can be performed. Intentions cannot be faked. Guests feel, through the phone, through the message, through the smile, whether the heart behind the action was present. So the discipline of ESTIA begins here.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"The thirty-second pause is a habit, not a rule. No tracking. It is a discipline you give yourself.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"No sales commissions. We do not tie compensation to closing deals \u2014 it corrupts intention. Bonuses are tied to guest return rates and referrals: if we host well, they come back.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Monthly Intention Reflection. On the last week of every month, the team gathers. Each person shares one action this month with pure intention, and one done merely to complete. No judgement. Only honesty.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 02 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"02\", \"Be a host.\", \"\u7576\u4e00\u500b\u4e3b\u4eba,\u4e0d\u662f\u54e1\u5de5\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"You are not coming to work; you are returning to a home you are entrusted with. A guest is not a customer; he is a friend you have invited over. The difference between an employee and a host is this: employees execute procedures. Hosts have judgement, warmth, and taste.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"WHY THIS MATTERS\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"The service industry in Taiwan too often trains employees into procedure-executing machines. Procedures set a floor but also a ceiling \u2014 the guest feels the polite distance. ESTIA reverses this. We give hosts the authority of an owner. You may use your judgement to welcome a guest \u2014 because this is your house.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Host's Discretion Budget. Every host has NT$5,000 per month to spend on guests without approval \u2014 flowers on a birthday, a book someone mentioned, a small dessert they praised. No paperwork. Once a month we share what we spent it on, and learn from each other.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Rotation into hosting and housekeeping. Every ESTIA team member \u2014 including the founder, finance, operations \u2014 spends two days per year as a host and one day as a housekeeper. If you have not folded the linens, you cannot decide what linens we buy.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Internal vocabulary. We do not use 'staff', 'employee', or 'customer' in internal communication. We use 'host' and the guest's name. Language shapes attitude; attitude shapes behaviour.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 03 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"03\", \"Genuine service.\", \"\u771f\u8aa0\u7684\u670d\u52d9,\u4e0d\u662f\u5c08\u696d\u7684\u8868\u6f14\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"'Genuine' is harder than 'professional', harder than 'warm', harder than 'attentive'. It cannot be performed. We do not train our hosts to smile. We hire people whose smile is already real, and we allow them to be themselves.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"WHY THIS MATTERS\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"The paradox of the hospitality industry: the more you train, the more artificial the service becomes. Because scripts turn people into performers. Our answer, like Aesop's, is to replace training with hiring \u2014 and to replace SOPs with values. If we have hired the right people, the right behaviour follows without instruction.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"THE INTERVIEW PROCESS\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Every host is chosen through a five-round process totalling roughly five and a half hours of assessment. This is our most important policy \u2014 and our most expensive protection against a bad hire.\"));\n\n// Interview details\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 1 \u00b7 THIRTY-MINUTE CONVERSATION\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: to feel the person's temperature and see whether he has a story to tell.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Three required questions:\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Tell me about a book or a film you have enjoyed recently.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Have you ever been welcomed somewhere with real warmth? What did that feel like?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"When you travel, what kind of place do you choose to stay in \u2014 and why?\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Watch for: pauses of thought, eyes lighting up, personal taste. Reject: rehearsed answers, refusal to share anything personal, resum\u00e9s instead of stories.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 2 \u00b7 HALF DAY ON SITE\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: let the candidate observe an ESTIA in operation, and eat one meal with the team.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Arrive at 10:00, tea, shadow a real check-in, watch a room inspection, then lunch with the team from 11:30 to 13:00. The lunch is the crucial hour.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Watch for: chemistry with the current team, curiosity about the space, small caring gestures during lunch. Reject: distance from the team, indifference to the environment, questions only about pay and benefits.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 3 \u00b7 YOUR PLACE\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: to see the candidate's taste and how he hosts you.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Ask him to choose a caf\u00e9, bookshop, or bar he loves, and meet him there. He is now in his territory. Watch how he introduces the place, whether the staff know him, whether he orders for you, whether he tells you what to try.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Three questions to ask over coffee:\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Why did you choose here?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"What kind of service do you dislike?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"A hotel or restaurant you will never return to \u2014 what happened?\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 4 \u00b7 JUDGEMENT SCENARIOS\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: to observe reasoning, empathy, and where he draws lines.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"There are no correct answers. Only the quality of thinking matters.\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"Scenario A \u2014 2 a.m. request. A guest messages at 2 a.m.: he has forgotten his suit for an 8 a.m. meeting. What do you do?\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"Scenario B \u2014 Quiet change. A European guest has stayed three months, always warm and talkative. This week you notice he stops going out, his eyes look tired, and he no longer greets you. What do you do?\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"Scenario C \u2014 Boundaries. The housekeeper mentions she saw what looked like antidepressants on a guest's desk. What do you do?\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"Reject: candidates who give textbook answers with no thought, who are cold, who overreact and want to 'rescue', who cannot distinguish care from intrusion.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 5 \u00b7 WITH THE FOUNDER\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: intuition, and passing on the soul of the brand.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Jim shares the Origin Story. He explains why ESTIA exists. He asks: 'After hearing all this, would you want to be here for five years? For ten?'\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Then Jim asks himself three private questions:\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Would I let this person host my closest friend on his behalf?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Am I comfortable letting this person represent me to a guest?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"If, in five years, this person is still at ESTIA, would I be glad?\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Three yeses \u2014 hire. One no \u2014 regardless of every other strength \u2014 do not hire.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"SUPPORTING POLICIES\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"No service scripts. ESTIA does not issue standard greetings, complaint-handling phrases, or approved sales language. We give hosts values and context; they choose their own words.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Honest opinions. If a guest asks your view on a restaurant, you may honestly say another one is better. Yes to everything is not warmth \u2014 it is dishonesty.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 04 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"04\", \"Every detail is a welcome.\", \"\u6bcf\u4e00\u500b\u7d30\u7bc0,\u90fd\u662f\u4e00\u6b21\u6b61\u8fce\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"A message without a typo, a reply within a minute, a doorframe at the right proportion, a cup of tea at the right temperature \u2014 these are not small things. Every overlooked detail is us telling the world 'this is good enough for us.' The difference between an excellent team and an ordinary one is not talent; it is tolerance for imperfection.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Zero-typo culture. Every outgoing message \u2014 email, LINE, SMS, contract \u2014 is read aloud once before sending. A typo is not a small error; it is evidence you did not care enough to reread.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"The Guest's Eye walk-through. Thirty minutes before every arrival, the responsible host walks through the room 'as the guest': what he sees when the door opens, where his shoes go, whether the light warms the room, whether the mirror is fingerprint-free, whether a single hair lies on the bathroom floor.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Friday Detail Circle. Every Friday afternoon, thirty minutes. Each host shares one imperfection they noticed this week and how they fixed it. Not for blame \u2014 for eye-training. Only those who see imperfection can eliminate it.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 05 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"05\", \"Anticipate, don't apologise.\", \"\u4e3b\u52d5\u9810\u898b,\u800c\u975e\u4e8b\u5f8c\u9053\u6b49\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"The moment a guest opens his mouth to complain, ESTIA has already lost points. A great host is not one who fixes problems quickly \u2014 but one who prevents them from occurring.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Guest Profile system. Every guest's preferences, family names, dietary restrictions, birthdays, prior comments and prior compliments live in a shared system \u2014 not in one host's head. So the next stay, whoever receives him, resumes without a beat.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"48-hour arrival brief. Forty-eight hours before arrival, the responsible host holds a fifteen-minute internal review: revisit the guest's profile, forecast his likely needs, and prepare three small anticipations he never asked for.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Complaints as system failures. A guest complaint is not the host's mistake \u2014 it is our system's. Post-complaint review asks 'where did our anticipation break?' \u2014 never 'whose fault?' Hosts stop fearing complaints and start learning to see ahead.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 06 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"06\", \"Cultural literacy.\", \"\u6587\u5316\u7d20\u990a,\u662f\u6b3e\u5f85\u7684\u5e95\u6c23\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Our guests come to Taiwan not only to stay well \u2014 they come to know Taiwan. A host who can only explain the room is a servant. A host who can talk about Hou Hsiao-hsien's films, the history of Longshan Temple, the difference between the old tea houses of Dadaocheng \u2014 he is a translator of Taipei's culture. Our guests fall in love with the city because of him.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Estia Reads \u2014 Book of the Month. Every month, ESTIA gifts every host a book. Taiwan's history one month, architecture the next, then cinema, cuisine, contemporary fiction, travel writing. The last week of the month, we sit together with coffee and share what we read. Not a book club \u2014 a cultivation of taste.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Estia Explores \u2014 Quarterly Discovery. Once a quarter, each host receives one paid day and NT$3,000 to visit one place in Taipei \u2014 an old tea shop, an independent bookstore, a gallery, a historic neighbourhood. He writes one page for the team. Your taste becomes ours.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"The host's own walks. Each host prepares at least three Taipei walking routes he has personally walked and can genuinely recommend. Not Google's answers; his answers. When a guest asks 'where should I go?', he does not reach for his phone \u2014 he is the map.\"));\n\nchildren.push(divider());\n\nchildren.push(spacer(400));\nchildren.push(body(\"The final two values do not belong to hosts. They belong to the founder \u2014 the disciplines a founder must protect on behalf of the brand, even against pressure to grow or economise.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 07 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"07\", \"Warmth needs the right bones.\", \"\u6eab\u5ea6,\u9577\u5728\u5c0d\u7684\u9aa8\u67b6\u4e0a\u3002 (Founder-guarded)\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"We pursue warmth \u2014 but not through sentiment. Before colour, before furniture, before decor: the length of the wall, the proportion of the door, the ratio of the ceiling, the raw form of the material. Get the bones right first, and warmth has somewhere to live.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"WHY THIS IS FOUNDER-GUARDED\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Because these decisions are made by Jim personally. Design direction, material selection, spatial ratios \u2014 none of these are delegated. This value protects the discipline that keeps ESTIA's spaces from drifting toward trend or economy.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Raw material principle. All materials used in ESTIA properties must be in raw form: solid wood, real stone, real metal, real leather. No veneer, no composite, no imitation. Even when costs double, we hold this line.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Proportion first. Every design review begins with proportion \u2014 never colour, furniture, or lighting. Door length, wall ratios, ceiling height, floor seam direction. Bones first, then skin.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"The three-year test. Every soft furnishing, piece of furniture, and amenity is selected against one question: will this still look right in three years? If the answer is uncertain, we do not choose it.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 08 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"08\", \"Fewer, deeper.\", \"\u5c11,\u4f46\u6df1\u3002 (Founder-guarded)\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"We would rather run ten residences whose hosts know every guest by name than fifty that lose their temperature. Quality decides how large we grow \u2014 never the market.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"WHY THIS IS FOUNDER-GUARDED\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Because this is the discipline the market will constantly push against. Investors will ask why not more properties. Landlords will offer good buildings in wrong neighbourhoods. Growth is easy; restraint is hard. It falls to the founder to hold this line.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Eight-unit host ceiling. No host is responsible for more than eight units. Beyond that, one cannot remember every guest's name, birthday, and preference.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Prime districts only. We operate only in Taipei's core neighbourhoods. Even at triple the cost, we do not extend into second-tier areas. Eight policies.\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"What follows is the constitution of ESTIA Residences. Six values for every team member. Two values for the founder to safeguard on behalf of the brand. These tell you what you would want to do \u2014 if you understood who we are.\"));\n\nchildren.push(divider());\n\nchildren.push(body(\"The first six values are for every host. They shape how we think, decide, and speak \u2014 every day, in every interaction.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 01 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"01\", \"Intention over action.\", \"\u610f\u5716,\u5148\u65bc\u52d5\u4f5c\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"What guests feel is not what we do \u2014 it is why we do it. Before every call, every message, every arrival, pause for thirty seconds and ask yourself one question: am I doing this to help him, or to get it done? Those thirty seconds are the difference between ESTIA and everyone else.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"WHY THIS IS FIRST\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Every other value is an action \u2014 smile, remember, respond, notice. Actions can be performed. Intentions cannot be faked. Guests feel, through the phone, through the message, through the smile, whether the heart behind the action was present. So the discipline of ESTIA begins here.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"The thirty-second pause is a habit, not a rule. No tracking. It is a discipline you give yourself.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"No sales commissions. We do not tie compensation to closing deals \u2014 it corrupts intention. Bonuses are tied to guest return rates and referrals: if we host well, they come back.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Monthly Intention Reflection. On the last week of every month, the team gathers. Each person shares one action this month with pure intention, and one done merely to complete. No judgement. Only honesty.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 02 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"02\", \"Be a host.\", \"\u7576\u4e00\u500b\u4e3b\u4eba,\u4e0d\u662f\u54e1\u5de5\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"You are not coming to work; you are returning to a home you are entrusted with. A guest is not a customer; he is a friend you have invited over. The difference between an employee and a host is this: employees execute procedures. Hosts have judgement, warmth, and taste.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"WHY THIS MATTERS\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"The service industry in Taiwan too often trains employees into procedure-executing machines. Procedures set a floor but also a ceiling \u2014 the guest feels the polite distance. ESTIA reverses this. We give hosts the authority of an owner. You may use your judgement to welcome a guest \u2014 because this is your house.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"POLICY\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Host's Discretion Budget. Every host has NT$5,000 per month to spend on guests without approval \u2014 flowers on a birthday, a book someone mentioned, a small dessert they praised. No paperwork. Once a month we share what we spent it on, and learn from each other.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Rotation into hosting and housekeeping. Every ESTIA team member \u2014 including the founder, finance, operations \u2014 spends two days per year as a host and one day as a housekeeper. If you have not folded the linens, you cannot decide what linens we buy.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Internal vocabulary. We do not use 'staff', 'employee', or 'customer' in internal communication. We use 'host' and the guest's name. Language shapes attitude; attitude shapes behaviour.\"));\n\n// \u2500\u2500 VALUE 03 \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\nvalueHeader(\"03\", \"Genuine service.\", \"\u771f\u8aa0\u7684\u670d\u52d9,\u4e0d\u662f\u5c08\u696d\u7684\u8868\u6f14\u3002\").forEach(p => children.push(p));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"MEANING\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"'Genuine' is harder than 'professional', harder than 'warm', harder than 'attentive'. It cannot be performed. We do not train our hosts to smile. We hire people whose smile is already real, and we allow them to be themselves.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"WHY THIS MATTERS\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"The paradox of the hospitality industry: the more you train, the more artificial the service becomes. Because scripts turn people into performers. Our answer, like Aesop's, is to replace training with hiring \u2014 and to replace SOPs with values. If we have hired the right people, the right behaviour follows without instruction.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"THE INTERVIEW PROCESS\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Every host is chosen through a five-round process totalling roughly five and a half hours of assessment. This is our most important policy \u2014 and our most expensive protection against a bad hire.\"));\n\n// Interview details\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 1 \u00b7 THIRTY-MINUTE CONVERSATION\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: to feel the person's temperature and see whether he has a story to tell.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Three required questions:\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Tell me about a book or a film you have enjoyed recently.\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Have you ever been welcomed somewhere with real warmth? What did that feel like?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"When you travel, what kind of place do you choose to stay in \u2014 and why?\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Watch for: pauses of thought, eyes lighting up, personal taste. Reject: rehearsed answers, refusal to share anything personal, resum\u00e9s instead of stories.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 2 \u00b7 HALF DAY ON SITE\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: let the candidate observe an ESTIA in operation, and eat one meal with the team.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Arrive at 10:00, tea, shadow a real check-in, watch a room inspection, then lunch with the team from 11:30 to 13:00. The lunch is the crucial hour.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Watch for: chemistry with the current team, curiosity about the space, small caring gestures during lunch. Reject: distance from the team, indifference to the environment, questions only about pay and benefits.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 3 \u00b7 YOUR PLACE\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: to see the candidate's taste and how he hosts you.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Ask him to choose a caf\u00e9, bookshop, or bar he loves, and meet him there. He is now in his territory. Watch how he introduces the place, whether the staff know him, whether he orders for you, whether he tells you what to try.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Three questions to ask over coffee:\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Why did you choose here?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"What kind of service do you dislike?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"A hotel or restaurant you will never return to \u2014 what happened?\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 4 \u00b7 JUDGEMENT SCENARIOS\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: to observe reasoning, empathy, and where he draws lines.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"There are no correct answers. Only the quality of thinking matters.\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"Scenario A \u2014 2 a.m. request. A guest messages at 2 a.m.: he has forgotten his suit for an 8 a.m. meeting. What do you do?\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"Scenario B \u2014 Quiet change. A European guest has stayed three months, always warm and talkative. This week you notice he stops going out, his eyes look tired, and he no longer greets you. What do you do?\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"Scenario C \u2014 Boundaries. The housekeeper mentions she saw what looked like antidepressants on a guest's desk. What do you do?\"));\n\nchildren.push(body(\"Reject: candidates who give textbook answers with no thought, who are cold, who overreact and want to 'rescue', who cannot distinguish care from intrusion.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"ROUND 5 \u00b7 WITH THE FOUNDER\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Purpose: intuition, and passing on the soul of the brand.\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Jim shares the Origin Story. For ten?'\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Then Jim asks himself three private questions:\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Would I let this person host my closest friend on his behalf?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"Am I comfortable letting this person represent me to a guest?\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"If, in five years, this person is still at ESTIA, would I be glad?\"));\nchildren.push(body(\"Three yeses \u2014 hire. One no \u2014 regardless of every other strength \u2014 do not hire.\"));\n\nchildren.push(label(\"SUPPORTING POLICIES\"));\nchildren.push(bullet(\"No service scripts.
